## Document 083: 2408南亞科 元富

Source PDF: all report pdf/2408南亞科 元富.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

本刊載之報告為元富投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效性，
審慎考量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬元富投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。 
元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號 
公司拜訪快報

南亞科(2408) 
建議：維持買進 
結論：2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成

一、南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造廠，2026 年資本支出
上修至 697 億元(YoY+420%) 
南亞科為利基型 DRAM 製造商 ， 最大股東為台塑集團之
南亞塑膠持有 29.28%股權，主要競爭對手包含
Samsung、SK Hynix、Micron 以及華邦電，目前月產能
為 6.5 萬片，20nm 和 1B 為主力生產製程。依產品終端
應用區分，消費性電子和 AI 分別占營收比重為 60%+和
20%+。公司維持 2026 年銷售量年增 15-20%目標，因
5A 新廠需待 2H27 量產且存貨處於低水位 ， 故銷售量年
增率不如去年亮眼。至於在資本支出方面，2026 年資本
支出由 520 億元上修至 697 億元，其中五成將用於生產
設備 。 此外 ， 新廠將於1Q27 開始裝機 ，2H27 開始量產，
2028 年達成產能 30K， 新廠全產能由45K 上修至 50K，
投入資金為 4800 億元。 
二、客戶新 LTA 生效致使 ASP 季增幅超過六成，3Q26 
25.4 元續創新高 
3Q26 營收 1336 億元(+62%QoQ)，受惠於 LTA 長約價
格調漲，ASP 季增超過六十位數百分比，銷售量和匯率
則分別為季減中個位數和季增低個位數 ， 受惠於ASP 大
幅上揚且折舊費用持穩 ， 毛利率85.7 優於前季之 79.5%，
營業利益 1068.9 億元(+76%QoQ)，稅後淨利 876.2 億
元(+75%QoQ)，EPS 25.4 元續創新高。 
三、DRAM 供不應求延續，但 ASP 增幅將收斂，估計
4Q26 EPS 28.13 元 
DRAM 產業供不應求持續 ， 原廠供給持續向AI 傾斜 ， 由
於合約價已大幅調漲，ASP 增幅將收斂，估計 4Q26 營
收 1474.1 億元(+10.3%QoQ)，毛利率改善至 86.6%，
營業利益 1196.5 億元(+12%QoQ)，稅後淨利 970.4 億
元(+11%QoQ)，EPS 28.13 元。  
四、原廠新產能規劃搭配客戶長約需求，且 HBM 投片
占比增加，2027 年產業供不應求已成定局  
在產業需求方面，全球九大 CSP 2027 年 Capex 1.38
兆美元(+50%YoY)，AI 運算將主導 DRAM 需求成長，
帶動 DRAM 需求從 HBM、LPDDR5 擴展至網路 、 儲存，
至於傳統應用則面臨供給不足以及價格調漲，導致產品
降規或生命周期延長，部分低階市場需求則需仰賴回收
DRAM。在產業供給方面，雖供應商整體擴充產能，但
產能規劃將搭配客戶長約需求，有助長期供需穩定，加
計 HBM 於產品投片占比由今年 22%增加至明年之 30%，
致使 DRAM 新增產能主要用於 HBM 生產。故以整體市
況而言，AI 強勁需求將推動記憶體供需結構改變，降低
景氣循環對於供應商影響，產業庫存仍偏低，2027 年產
業供不應求可望延續。 
在 DRAM 報價走勢上 ， 雖消費端壓力未減 ， 但AI Server
增量需求支撐 4Q26 記憶體合約價漲勢 ， 集邦預估4Q26
傳統型 DRAM 價格季增 10-15%低於前季之 13-18%，
就南亞科而言 ， 營收占比達50%+的 LTA 客戶已於 3Q26
大幅調漲價格，故預期 4Q26 價格漲勢將趨緩。 
五、新廠產出效益於 2H27 開始發酵，長期 AI 產能占比
上看五成 
南亞科今年資本支出從 520 億元上修至 697 億元，主因
為生產設備投資增加，5A 新廠維持 1Q27 裝機規劃，
2028 年和 2030 年投片目標分別為 30K 和 50K， 整體新
廠投資規模達 4800 億元 ， 由於5A 新廠量產預計於 2H27，
故 4Q26-2Q27 出貨量約當 3Q26，至於屏東 3D 晶圓封
裝測試廠預期在 2028 年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)和晶圓堆
疊等先進技術。此外，儘管資本支出積極，目前AI 基礎
設施(包含 Server、網路、儲存以及系統管理)客戶營收
占比超過 20%， 但新產能擴建主要以AI 客戶需求為主，
故經營階層預期 2029-2030 年 AI 營收占比將上看
40-50%。 
六、維持買進理由 
1)預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 75.63 元和
119.26 元。 
2)考量 2027 年產業供需吃緊不變，長期 AI 營運占比挑
戰五成，維持買進評等，TP 660 元 (2027 PBR x 2.8)。

## Document 120: 261027_daiwa_faraday

Source PDF: all report pdf/261027_daiwa_faraday.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Faraday released a mixed set of results and guidance on 27 
October. 3Q26 core results missed consensus on an unfavourable product 
mix and higher opex, while 4Q26 guidance was broadly intact on delayed 
MCU business rebalancing. Regardless of the near-term hiccup, 
management expects its 3 product lines of mass-production (MP), IP and 
non-recurrent engineering (NRE) to fire on all cylinders into 2027 for a 
double-digit % YoY revenue growth. This concurs with our turnaround story 
for the counter, and we reiterate our Buy (1) rating and 12M TP of TWD220.

What’s the impact: Results mixed, guidance intact. Faraday’s 3Q26 net 
profit came in at TWD279m, 9% above our estimate and beating 
consensus by a meaningful 38%. Despite the beat, dynamics were mixed 
(table on p.2). Core operations missed despite a higher top line, which was 
spurred by MP on stronger-than-expected MCU sales from its 50%-owned 
Artery (6907 TT, not rated) for a lower gross margin (GM) due to an 
unfavourable revenue mix as MP carries a lower GM than the other 2 lines. 
Higher opex, as a result of salary hikes and employee bonuses, further 
pressured core operations with operating profit missing our/consensus 
estimates by 36%/11%, but non-operating gains from marked-to-market 
investments more than offset the miss. Guidance calls for a roughly flat 
4Q26 top line with a 45% GM, broadly in line with our expectation.

Firing on all cylinders across all 3 lines. In our previous note, we 
expected rebalancing in the MCU business due to demand pull-in on price 
hikes and supply chain uncertainties. It failed to happen in 3Q26 thanks to 
early pull-in of OSAT capacity support, thus pushing back the rebalancing 
to 4Q26 — a key reason why top line was guided to be flat despite the 
other 2 lines of IP and NRE growing QoQ. That said, management expects 
the MCU business to return to a growth track in 2027 after the near-term 
hiccup. Its delayed advanced packaging (AP) ramp on design complexity 
and supply constraint looks set to accelerate in 2027 on OSAT capacity 
improvement which, together with a milestone of 2nm/GAA design-win for a 
cloud ASIC, should add to upside for the NRE and MP business next year. 
All in, we lower our 2026-27E EPS by 9-13% to factor in the higher opex.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we keep our 12M 
TP for Faraday unchanged at TWD220 as we maintain our 4-quarter 
forward PER target of 37x. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with key 
downside risks of silicon inflation and geopolitical overhangs leading to 
global inflation and prolonging a non-AI recovery.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 6-9% below the consensus 
forecasts likely on the street’s lower opex assumptions.

27 October 2026

Turnaround confirmed (II)

➢ 3Q26 results a mixed bag; 4Q26 guidance broadly intact 
➢ Firing on all cylinders into 2027 after near-term hiccups 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) call and 12M TP of TWD220

Company profile 
Founded in 1993, Faraday is a hybrid design service provider offering IP and ASIC design services to 
fabless, foundry and system houses. It has R&D and marketing locations in the US, Japan, China, India, 
and Vietnam.

### Page 5

5

Faraday Technology (3035 TT): 27 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 10 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Faraday’s Board of Directors (BoD) consists of 10 members, including 4 independent directors,
representing 40% of the board—significantly above the regulatory minimum of 20%. Three of the
10 directors are female, reflecting progress toward gender diversity at the board level. The roles
of Chairman and President are separated, supporting stronger governance checks and
balances. However, most non independent directors concurrently serve as executive managers,
which may elevate concerns regarding board independence and management oversight from an
ESG governance perspective.
Capital management 1
Over the past 10 years, Faraday has kept its dividend payout ratio at above 70% most of the
time, in spite of an up or down cycle. As an IC design service company without capex for
manufacturing fabs, Faraday's high dividend payout is appropriate, in our view.
Related party &
transaction 1
UMC, which is Faraday's largest shareholder, is Faraday's major foundry for its turnkey
business. For IC design service companies, a close relationship with foundries is essential since
their design should build on a foundry's process node and their turnkey businesses also require
a foundry's capacity support. UMC's support offers Faraday a competitive advantage, in our
view. Faraday has developed many standard cell and I/O libraries for UMC, covering logic and
mixed-signal specialty processes across 0.5μ to 22nm.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Faraday has set several evaluation standards for its existing and new suppliers and it requires all
suppliers to get ISO9001 and ISO14001 certifications for quality and environmental
management. In 2024, 100% of its wafer, packaging and testing providers had ISO9001 and
ISO14001 certifications. In addition, Faraday also has on-site audits (quality system and process
quality audit, CSR evaluation and environment management system) for its suppliers with a mid-
to-long term target of conducting annual onsite audits for 100% of its suppliers.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 1
As an IC design service company, Faraday outsources its manufacturing and thus has no own
production sites. As such, Faraday's water use is primarily for general operations, not for
production. Faraday continues to conduct several water reduction measures to manage water
usage.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
Faraday has established a green supply chain in accordance with the EU Directive on the
Restriction of the Use of Certain Hazardous Substances in Electrical and Electronic Equipment
(RoHS) and with the EU Chemicals Policy (REACH).
Faraday has a mid-to-long term target of carefully evaluating and selecting raw materials with
low hazard levels to comply with the Hazardous Substance-Free Product Standard. All of its
packaging materials are 100% lead-free and halogen-free.
E GHG emissions 1
With Faraday's energy saving measures, it reduced the GHG emissions intensity gradually. As
an IC design service company without onsite production, Faraday's GHG emissions are much
lower than other semiconductor manufacturing companies. Faraday's low power design also
helps reduce GHG emissions. In 2019, Faraday completed the development of its ultra-low
power consumption 22nm IP solution.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

股價相對大盤走勢

長榮航太 (2645 TT)  Trading Buy

預期波音 737 系列交機速度將略為放緩。  
投資建議 ：雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量
波音將維持 737 系列月均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太
的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航太 Trading Buy 的投資建
議。  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造
的營運預估： 波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會
上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時
間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到 2H27 才會上升至 52
架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時間，故研
究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
3Q26 先前的維修漲價陸續貢獻，營運維持高檔： 3Q26 長榮航
太自 2H24 起陸續針對維修業務進行漲價後，加上全球機隊持
續供不應求，增加維修機率與維修產值，惟製造部門雖然波音
已將 737 系列交機上升至 47 架 /月，但短期拉貨力道仍有限，
使得長榮航太的製造業務與轉投資公司營運維持低檔，營運仍
以維修業務為主， 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元， QoQ+1.02% ，預估稅
後純益 8.31 億元， QoQ+10.34% ， EPS 2.22 元。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營
運新動能： 2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機
的逐年擴張之外，在新訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，
預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待後續取得特殊製程認證後，
2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單也有望成為長榮
航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ，EPS 10.04 元。

3 群益投顧 
圖一、全球商用飛機平均機齡

資料來源 : IATA ，長榮航太  
波音 737 系列月交機將維持一段時間，調整長榮航太後續製造的營運預估：
波音於 10/27/2026 公告 3Q26 財報，並於法說會上提及 :「當前需維持 737 系
列月均交機量穩定在 47 架一段時間，後續管理階層預期交機提升可能要到
2H27 才會上升至 52 架」，使得長榮航太的製造部門復甦仍需等待一段時
間，故研究部一併調整長榮航太後續的營運預估。  
2027 年空中巴士首件交付、無人機新訂單，有望成為中長期營運新動能：  
2027 年長榮航太除了持續受惠維修業務與波音交機的逐年擴張之外，在新
訂單的部分，先前與空中巴士的新訂單，預估 1Q27 將迎來首件交付，並待
後續取得特殊製程認證後， 2H27 有望迎來新訂單的量產，以及無人機訂單
也有望成為長榮航太新一波的營運動能，預估 2027 年營收 224.86 億元，
YoY+7.85% ，稅後純益 37.61 億元， YoY+17.06% ， EPS 10.04 元。  
投資建議：  
雖然 2026 年長榮航太的維修業務持續擴張，但考量波音將維持 737 系列月
均交機一段時間，可能會影響長榮航太的製造部門復甦情形，故給予長榮航
太 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

7 群益投顧

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 190: 63亿美元！币安遭“史上最大”黑客洗劫，暂停全部存储和提取功能，承诺将承担所有损失

Source PDF: all report pdf/63亿美元！币安遭“史上最大”黑客洗劫，暂停全部存储和提取功能，承诺将承担所有损失.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

币安创始⼈赵⻓鹏已公开确认攻击属实，但强调⽤⼾资⾦安全。币安最新宣布，估计总影
响⾦额约为 63 亿美元。 TRM 全球政策负责⼈ Ari Redbord 称其为 “ ⼈类历史上最⼤的银⾏抢
劫案 ” 。值得注意的是，此次事件发⽣于 " ⿊⾊⼗⽉ " ⽹络攻击浪潮期间， AI 技术催⽣新型攻
击⼿段被认为是重要背景因素。
摘要：
全球最⼤加密货币交易所币安周四遭遇史上规模最⼤的加密货币⿊客攻击，被迫暂停全平台存
取款业务，事件震动整个加密市场。此次⿊客攻击造成的损失约达 63 亿美元，远超此前任何⼀
起同类事件。币安称，估计总影响⾦额约为 63 亿美元。
据彭博最先报道，此次⼤规模资⾦异动引发市场⾼度警觉。币安创始⼈赵⻓鹏 10 ⽉ 29 ⽇在社交
平台 X 上发⽂确认， “ 看起来确实是针对币安的⿊客攻击 ”，但同时强调⽤⼾资⾦安全。稳定币发
⾏商 Tether 和 Circle 随即采取紧急⾏动，合计冻结逾 15 亿美元与此次事件相关的资产，以限制潜
在损失蔓延。币安最新宣布，经核实，冻结资⾦总额约 30 亿美元。

币安随后在 X 平台发⽂宣布，将承担所有损失，并已暂停全部存储和提取功能，同时与执法机关
展开合作，承诺调查完成后将公布完整的事后调查报告。若最终确认，此次事件将打破 2025 年
朝鲜⿊客从 Bybit 盗取 15 亿美元所创下的加密货币史上最⼤劫案纪录。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

史上最⼤⽹络劫案
此次针对币安的攻击规模史⽆前例。华尔街⽇报称， TRM 全球政策负责⼈ Ari Redbord 称其为 “ ⼈
类历史上最⼤的银⾏抢劫案 ” 。
据币安在 X 平台发表的声明，初步调查显⽰，此次攻击涉及 “ ⼀名 DevOps ⼯程师的笔记本电脑
遭到⼊侵 ” ，攻击者借此得以 " 提交未经授权的提款交易 " 。
据华尔街⽇报，这⼀攻击路径揭⽰了加密货币交易所⾯临的结构性脆弱性。与证券交易所不
同，许多加密货币交易所直接代客⼾托管资产，将⼤量资⾦集中存放于加密钱包中，使其成为
⿊客的⾼价值⽬标。
在币安公布 63 亿美元的⾦额之前，华尔街⽇报报道指出，区块链安全机构 SEAL 911 的 Taylor
Monahan 表⽰，攻击者实际获利“ 远超 20 亿美元 ” ，最⾼可能达 55 亿美元。 “ 信息变化很快，确认
确切数字还需要时间， ”Monahan 说。华尔街⽇报联系的其他多位⽹络安全专家则将数字定在 55
亿美元以上。
据 Axios 报道，密切追踪加密市场的分析师和交易员估计，此次疑似⽹络攻击中被盗⾦额介于 17

亿⾄ 57 亿美元之间。

### Page 3

Tether ⾸席执⾏官 Paolo Ardoino 在 X 上发⽂称，该公司已冻结逾 15.67 亿美元的数字货币，原因
是 “ 与币安交易所潜在⿊客攻击相关 ” 。 Circle ⾸席执⾏官 Jeremy Allaire 则表⽰， Circle 已配合执
法部⻔冻结逾 8 亿美元 USDC，并将持续⽀持调查。
报道称， Tether 和 Circle 的快速介⼊，在⼀定程度上遏制了损失进⼀步蔓延。赵⻓鹏最初援引的
被冻结资产规模为约 17 亿美元，币安随后将这⼀数字修正为跨多类资产共计约 30 亿美元。
稳定币发⾏⽅冻结资产的机制是：将⿊客的加密钱包地址列⼊⿊名单，使被盗代币⽆法移动、
转账或套现，从⽽在⿊客将其兑换为其他加密货币或通过混币器洗钱之前将资⾦锁定。
币安回应：⽤⼾资⾦安全，将承担全部损失
⾯对外界关切，币安⽅⾯给出了明确表态。
币安发⾔⼈在声明中表⽰： “ 我们的团队已知悉有关潜在安全问题的报告，正在积极调查。请放
⼼，⽤⼾资⾦完全安全。 “该发⾔⼈还表⽰，安全团队正在 “ 紧急审查所有相关信息 ” ，并提醒⽤

⼾ “ 对诈骗保持警惕 “ 。
币安随后在 X 平台进⼀步宣布，公司将承担所有损失，⽬前正不间断处理此事，⼒求解决问题，
并在安全检查期间暂停了所有存储和提取功能，同时与执法机关保持合作。调查完成后，币安
承诺将公布完整的事后调查报告。
赵⻓鹏虽已于 2023 年卸任币安⾸席执⾏官，⽬前仍为公司最⼤股东。他在 X 上写道： “ 作为股
东，我预期所有⽤⼾资⾦将得到全额覆盖。 ” 、

规模虽⼤，币安抗压能⼒相对较强
尽管事件规模空前，市场分析⼈⼠认为币安凭借⾃⾝体量，具备相对较强的抗冲击能⼒。

“ 如果这件事发⽣在加密⾏业任何其他公司⾝上，将是灾难性的， ”March 说。他指出，币安的规
模使其具备更强的隔离能⼒，可通过保险和资产负债表覆盖潜在损失，但加密市场价格的⼴泛
波动仍有可能发⽣。

其天然成为⿊客的⾼价值攻击⽬标。此外，存放在币安等平台的资⾦并不受政府存款保险保
障，任何赔付均取决于交易所⾃⾝的意愿和能⼒。
此次事件并⾮孤⽴发⽣，⽽是近期⼀系列⾼级别⽹络攻击浪潮的组成部分。
据 Axios 报道，此次疑似⿊客攻击发⽣于所谓“ ⿊⾊⼗⽉ ”期间 —— ⼀系列⿊客攻击和 AI 发现的漏
洞接连触发，引发外界对全球性⽹络安全危机的担忧。此次币安事件发⽣的数⼩时前，去中⼼
化预测市场平台 Polymarket 刚刚确认遭遇⽹络攻击，被迫暂停交易约⼀⼩时，攻击者⾄少卷⾛
270 万美元⽤⼾资⾦。
安全研究机构 Hudson Rock 将 Polymarket 事件与第三⽅⽹站监控⼯具 DebugBear 的数据泄露相
关联，后者当天早些时候已宣布正在应对⾃⾝的安全事故。
NBC 新闻指出，此次币安⼤规模资⾦异动发⽣之际，全球⽹络安全防线正处于⾼度戒备状态，
原因正是近期⼈⼯智能技术的快速进步催⽣了新型攻击⼿段的出现。

## Document 215: [000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

## Document 230: [005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업.pdf

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 
목표주가 500,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

자료: 현대차, 대신증권 Research Center

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

### Page 6

현대차(005380)

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망 
- 일회성 요인보다는 법인별 손익 믹스 및 적용 세율 차이에 따른 영향 
- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0.pdf

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

## Document 327: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 26-30

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 26-30.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30
Date:    39 minutes ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 26-30
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 24 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.5% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 0.7%, both
posting second straight weekly losses. The Nasdaq’s 2.9% slide on
Thursday was its biggest one-day drop since June, as concerns flared
over the cost and availability of financial capital for AI investment. The
price of WTI crude topped $100 a barrel amid US-Iran conflict concerns,
while the 10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.0%.
The FOMC meeting (Wed) is the focus this week, alongside the release of

Q3 GDP and September's PCED inflation report (Thu). San Francisco Fed
President Mary Daly and St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem speak
on Friday. A heavy week of Q3 earnings reports rounds out a packed
calendar. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC. The FOMC’s two-day meeting concludes Wednesday, followed
by Fed Chair Kevin Warsh’s press conference. The CME FedWatch Tool
puts the odds of a hike this week at 9% and the odds of one in December
at 84%. Federal funds futures imply 2.7 rate hikes over the next 12

months and 1.4 over the next six (chart). We believe the committee should
hike on Wednesday but won't because the midterm elections are so close.

### Page 4

(2) GDP & PCED. The first estimate of Q3 GDP (Thu) follows Q2’s third
estimate of 2.2% saar, with the GDP price index up 6.1%. The Atlanta
Fed’s GDPNow model estimates Q3 growth at 3.5% as of October 20, led
by a 17.8% jump in business equipment spending (chart).
September’s core PCED (Thu) follows August’s 3.0% y/y. That was well
below September’s 4.6% pace of core PPI final demand for personal
consumption and above the core CPI’s 2.6% (chart). The Cleveland Fed’s

Inflation Nowcasting model projects September’s core and headline PCED
at 3.1% and 3.6% y/y, up from August’s 3.0% and 3.4%. (The model’s
m/m rates are 0.28% and 0.47%.)
CPI computer software and accessories rose 28.4% y/y in September and
PPI electronic components 26.8%, while PCED computers and peripheral

### Page 5

equipment rose just 8.4% in August. September’s PCED will show
whether that gap starts to close.
(3) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a drop in the four-week average to 18,500 for the week
ended October 3, down from 26,000 the prior week (chart). That’s still
more than double July’s low of 8,250, pointing to a solid October payrolls
report.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 194,000, below expectations

and near the lowest level since the late 1960s. The four-week average
stands at 197,800 (chart). Continuing claims rose to 1,728,000 for the
week ended October 9, up from 1,710,000, while their four-week average
edged down to 1,708,000.

### Page 6

(4) Regional business surveys. The Richmond Fed’s manufacturing
index (Tue) follows September’s -2 reading. The regional M-PMI business
activity index slipped to 13.4 in September from August’s 18.7, while the
national M-PMI eased to 54.5 from 54.6 (chart).
(5) Earnings. The earnings reporting season heats up this week, with
36% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Boeing, Seagate, and
Corning report on Tuesday. Alphabet, Microsoft, SK Hynix, and GE

Vernova follow on Wednesday. Samsung, Intel, Eli Lilly, SanDisk, and
Amazon report on Thursday. Exxon Mobil reports on Friday.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 24.1% y/y, slightly up from last week (chart). Q4
estimates have also risen.

### Page 7

(6) Global central banks. Both the Reserve Bank of New Zealand and
the Bank of Canada (Wed) are expected to hike by 25bps. The markets
are pricing in 88% odds that the RBNZ will lift its official cash rate to
3.00% and an 85% chance that the BOC will raise its policy rate to 2.50%.
The European Central Bank (Thu) and the Bank of Japan (Fri) are set to
hold their policy rates at 2.50% and 1.25%, respectively (chart).
December is the focus for the latter pair. The markets are pricing in 75%

odds of an ECB hike in December and a 74% probability of a BOJ hike.
Guidance from ECB President Christine Lagarde and BOJ Governor
Kazuo Ueda will show whether those odds hold.

## Document 342: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

## Document 357: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

(%)
The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.

## Document 397: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the
